```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 2: LOAD DATASET & PROFESSIONAL PREPROCESSING
# =============================================================================

# Load the dataset
df = pd.read_csv('hotel_bookings.csv')

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("📊 DATASET LOADED SUCCESSFULLY")
print("="*80)
print(f"\n✅ Dataset Shape: {df.shape[0]:,} rows × {df.shape[1]} columns")
print(f"✅ Memory Usage: {df.memory_usage(deep=True).sum() / 1024**2:.2f} MB")

# Advanced preprocessing function from Notebook 2
def professional_preprocess(df):
    """
    Advanced preprocessing including type correction and
    feature engineering for hospitality metrics.
    """
    # Create a copy to prevent SettingWithCopyWarning
    data = df.copy()

    # Convert dates with error handling
    data['reservation_status_date'] = pd.to_datetime(data['reservation_status_date'])

    # Feature Engineering: Total Stay Duration
    data['total_nights'] = data['stays_in_weekend_nights'] + data['stays_in_week_nights']

    # Feature Engineering: Revenue Metric (Total Revenue)
    data['total_revenue'] = data['adr'] * data['total_nights']

    # Handle Nulls in critical columns
    data['children'] = data['children'].fillna(0)
    data['country'] = data['country'].fillna('Unknown')
    data['agent'] = data['agent'].fillna(0) # 0 means no agent

    # Filter out rows where total guests = 0 (Data anomalies)
    data = data[(data['adults'] + data['children'] + data['babies']) > 0]

    return data

# Apply professional preprocessing
df_clean = professional_preprocess(df)

print(f"\n✅ After preprocessing: {df_clean.shape[0]:,} rows × {df_clean.shape[1]} columns")
print("✅ Data quality issues resolved")
print("✅ Revenue metrics calculated")
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 3: ADVANCED OUTLIER MANAGEMENT (WINSORIZATION)
# =============================================================================

def handle_outliers_robustly(df, columns):
    """
    Instead of deleting data, we use Winsorization to cap
    extreme values at the 99th percentile to preserve statistical power.
    """
    for col in columns:
        upper_limit = df[col].quantile(0.99)
        lower_limit = df[col].quantile(0.01)
        df[col] = np.where(df[col] > upper_limit, upper_limit,
                          np.where(df[col] < lower_limit, lower_limit, df[col]))
    return df

# Apply robust outlier handling
print("🔧 Applying Winsorization to preserve data integrity...")
df_clean = handle_outliers_robustly(df_clean, ['adr', 'lead_time'])

print("✅ Outliers handled using 99th percentile capping")
print("✅ Statistical power preserved")

# Visualize before/after comparison
fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(15, 10))

# ADR distribution before
axes[0,0].hist(df['adr'], bins=50, alpha=0.7, color='red', label='Original')
axes[0,0].set_title('ADR Distribution - Before Winsorization')
axes[0,0].set_xlabel('Average Daily Rate (€)')
axes[0,0].set_ylabel('Frequency')

# ADR distribution after
axes[0,1].hist(df_clean['adr'], bins=50, alpha=0.7, color='green', label='Winsorized')
axes[0,1].set_title('ADR Distribution - After Winsorization')
axes[0,1].set_xlabel('Average Daily Rate (€)')
axes[0,1].set_ylabel('Frequency')

# Lead time before
axes[1,0].hist(df['lead_time'], bins=50, alpha=0.7, color='red')
axes[1,0].set_title('Lead Time Distribution - Before Winsorization')
axes[1,0].set_xlabel('Lead Time (Days)')
axes[1,0].set_ylabel('Frequency')

# Lead time after
axes[1,1].hist(df_clean['lead_time'], bins=50, alpha=0.7, color='green')
axes[1,1].set_title('Lead Time Distribution - After Winsorization')
axes[1,1].set_xlabel('Lead Time (Days)')
axes[1,1].set_ylabel('Frequency')

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('winsorization_comparison.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

print("💡 Key Insight: Winsorization preserves data points while reducing extreme value impact")
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 4: ADVANCED PORTFOLIO ANALYSIS ENGINE
# =============================================================================

def run_portfolio_analysis(df):
    """
    Advanced hospitality analytics combining multiple visualizations
    to reveal revenue and behavioral patterns.
    """
    # Set Professional Aesthetic
    sns.set_theme(style="whitegrid", palette="muted")
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(20, 15))
    fig.suptitle('Advanced Hospitality Analytics: Revenue & Behavioral Profile', fontsize=24)

    # --- A. ADR Distribution by Hotel Type (Violin Plot) ---
    # Shows density and interquartile ranges simultaneously
    sns.violinplot(x='hotel', y='adr', hue='is_canceled', data=df,
                   split=True, inner="quart", ax=axes[0, 0])
    axes[0, 0].set_title('Price Distribution vs Cancellation Impact', fontsize=16)

    # --- B. Lead Time vs. Cancellation (Probabilistic Analysis) ---
    # Using a KDE to show the "Danger Zone" for cancellations
    sns.kdeplot(data=df, x='lead_time', hue='is_canceled', fill=True, common_norm=False, ax=axes[0, 1])
    axes[0, 1].set_title('Lead Time Density: At what point do guests cancel?', fontsize=16)

    # --- C. Special Requests vs. ADR (Scatter with Regression) ---
    # Does paying more correlate with higher demands?
    sns.regplot(x='total_of_special_requests', y='adr', data=df, x_estimator=np.mean, ax=axes[1, 0], color='darkblue')
    axes[1, 0].set_title('Correlation: Price Point vs. Special Requests', fontsize=16)

    # --- D. Seasonality Analysis (Heatmap) ---
    month_order = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June',
                   'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December']
    pivot_table = df.pivot_table(index='arrival_date_month', columns='hotel', values='adr', aggfunc='mean').reindex(month_order)
    sns.heatmap(pivot_table, annot=True, fmt=".1f", cmap='YlGnBu', ax=axes[1, 1])
    axes[1, 1].set_title('Monthly ADR Seasonality Heatmap', fontsize=16)

    plt.tight_layout(rect=[0, 0.03, 1, 0.95])
    plt.show()

# Execute the portfolio analysis
print("🔍 Running advanced portfolio analysis...")
run_portfolio_analysis(df_clean)
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 5: INFERENTIAL STATISTICS & STATISTICAL TESTING
# =============================================================================

def statistical_tests(df):
    print("--- STATISTICAL SIGNIFICANCE REPORT ---")
    print("="*60)
    
    # Test 1: Is there a significant difference in ADR between canceled and non-canceled bookings?
    # Using Mann-Whitney U because data is non-normal
    group1 = df[df['is_canceled'] == 1]['adr']
    group2 = df[df['is_canceled'] == 0]['adr']
    stat, p = stats.mannwhitneyu(group1, group2)

    print(f"🎯 TEST 1: ADR vs Cancellation (Mann-Whitney U)")
    print(f"   p-value = {p:.6f}")
    if p < 0.05:
        print("   ✅ RESULT: Statistically Significant. Price is a driver of cancellation.")
        print(f"   📊 Mean ADR (Canceled): €{group1.mean():.2f}")
        print(f"   📊 Mean ADR (Not Canceled): €{group2.mean():.2f}")
    else:
        print("   ❌ RESULT: Not Significant.")
    print()

    # Test 2: Lead time difference between hotel types
    city_lead = df[df['hotel'] == 'City Hotel']['lead_time']
    resort_lead = df[df['hotel'] == 'Resort Hotel']['lead_time']
    stat2, p2 = stats.mannwhitneyu(city_lead, resort_lead)
    
    print(f"🎯 TEST 2: Lead Time by Hotel Type (Mann-Whitney U)")
    print(f"   p-value = {p2:.6f}")
    if p2 < 0.05:
        print("   ✅ RESULT: Significant difference in booking lead times.")
        print(f"   📊 Mean Lead Time (City): {city_lead.mean():.1f} days")
        print(f"   📊 Mean Lead Time (Resort): {resort_lead.mean():.1f} days")
    else:
        print("   ❌ RESULT: No significant difference.")
    print()

    # Test 3: Revenue difference between market segments
    segments = df['market_segment'].unique()
    revenue_by_segment = [df[df['market_segment'] == seg]['total_revenue'] for seg in segments]
    stat3, p3 = stats.kruskal(*revenue_by_segment)
    
    print(f"🎯 TEST 3: Revenue Difference Across Market Segments (Kruskal-Wallis)")
    print(f"   p-value = {p3:.6f}")
    if p3 < 0.05:
        print("   ✅ RESULT: Significant revenue differences across segments.")
        for seg in segments:
            seg_revenue = df[df['market_segment'] == seg]['total_revenue'].mean()
            print(f"   📊 {seg}: €{seg_revenue:.2f} average revenue")
    else:
        print("   ❌ RESULT: No significant revenue differences.")
    print("="*60)

# Execute statistical tests
print("🔬 Running comprehensive statistical analysis...")
statistical_tests(df_clean)
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 6: BUSINESS IMPACT ANALYSIS
# =============================================================================

def business_impact_analysis(df):
    """
    Calculates the financial impact of cancellations and identifies
    high-value customer segments.
    """
    print("\n" + "="*80)
    print("💰 BUSINESS IMPACT ANALYSIS")
    print("="*80)
    
    # 1. Opportunity Cost: Revenue Lost to Cancellations
    lost_revenue = df[df['is_canceled'] == 1]['total_revenue'].sum()
    realized_revenue = df[df['is_canceled'] == 0]['total_revenue'].sum()

    print(f"📊 Total Realized Revenue: €{realized_revenue:,.2f}")
    print(f"❌ Total Revenue Lost to Cancellations: €{lost_revenue:,.2f}")
    print(f"📈 Loss Ratio: {(lost_revenue / (lost_revenue + realized_revenue)) * 100:.2f}%")
    print("-" * 50)

    # 2. Behavioral Segment: The 'Reliable Premium' Guest
    # Defining premium as Top 25% ADR and 0 Cancellations
    adr_threshold = df['adr'].quantile(0.75)
    premium_reliable = df[(df['adr'] >= adr_threshold) & (df['is_canceled'] == 0)]

    print(f"🎯 HIGH-VALUE SEGMENT PROFILE (Premium Reliable Guests)")
    print(f"   💰 ADR Threshold: €{adr_threshold:.2f}")
    print(f"   👥 Segment Size: {len(premium_reliable):,} guests ({len(premium_reliable)/len(df)*100:.1f}%)")
    print(f"   📅 Average Lead Time: {premium_reliable['lead_time'].mean():.1f} days")
    print(f"   🍽️ Preferred Meal Plan: {premium_reliable['meal'].mode()[0] if not premium_reliable.empty else 'N/A'}")
    print(f"   ⭐ Avg Special Requests: {premium_reliable['total_of_special_requests'].mean():.2f}")
    print(f"   🏨 Preferred Hotel: {premium_reliable['hotel'].mode()[0] if not premium_reliable.empty else 'N/A'}")
    
    return lost_revenue, realized_revenue, premium_reliable

# Execute business impact analysis
print("🔍 Analyzing business impact and identifying high-value segments...")
lost_rev, realized_rev, premium_segment = business_impact_analysis(df_clean)

# Visualize the revenue impact
fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(16, 6))

# Revenue lost vs realized
revenue_data = pd.DataFrame({
    'Status': ['Realized Revenue', 'Lost Revenue (Cancellations)'],
    'Amount': [realized_rev, lost_rev],
    'Color': ['#2ECC71', '#E74C3C']
})

ax1 = axes[0]
bars = ax1.bar(revenue_data['Status'], revenue_data['Amount'], 
               color=revenue_data['Color'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
ax1.set_title('Revenue Impact Analysis', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold')
ax1.set_ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
ax1.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.EngFormatter(unit='€'))

# Add percentage labels
total = realized_rev + lost_rev
for i, (bar, pct) in enumerate(zip(bars, [realized_rev/total*100, lost_rev/total*100])):
    height = bar.get_height()
    ax1.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2., height + total*0.02,
             f'{pct:.1f}%\n€{height:,.0f}', ha='center', va='bottom', 
             fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)

# Premium segment characteristics
if not premium_segment.empty:
    premium_chars = {
        'Average ADR': premium_segment['adr'].mean(),
        'Average Lead Time': premium_segment['lead_time'].mean(),
        'Average Stay Duration': premium_segment['total_nights'].mean(),
        'Special Requests': premium_segment['total_of_special_requests'].mean()
    }
    
    ax2 = axes[1]
    metrics = list(premium_chars.keys())
    values = list(premium_chars.values())
    
    # Normalize values for better visualization
    normalized_values = [(v - min(values)) / (max(values) - min(values)) * 100 for v in values]
    
    bars2 = ax2.barh(metrics, normalized_values, color='#9B59B6', edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    ax2.set_title('Premium Reliable Guest Profile (Normalized)', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold')
    ax2.set_xlabel('Normalized Score (0-100)')
    
    # Add actual values as text
    for i, (bar, actual_val) in enumerate(zip(bars2, values)):
        width = bar.get_width()
        if 'ADR' in metrics[i]:
            label = f'€{actual_val:.0f}'
        elif 'Time' in metrics[i]:
            label = f'{actual_val:.0f} days'
        elif 'Duration' in metrics[i]:
            label = f'{actual_val:.1f} nights'
        else:
            label = f'{actual_val:.1f}'
        
        ax2.text(width + 2, bar.get_y() + bar.get_height()/2, 
                label, ha='left', va='center', fontweight='bold')

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('business_impact_analysis.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 7: ADVANCED REVENUE LOSS ANALYSIS
# =============================================================================

def analyze_revenue_loss(df):
    """
    Deep dive into revenue loss patterns, especially during peak seasons.
    """
    print("\n" + "="*80)
    print("📊 ADVANCED REVENUE LOSS ANALYSIS")
    print("="*80)
    
    # Filter for Summer Peak (July & August)
    summer_peak = df[df['arrival_date_month'].isin(['July', 'August'])]

    lost_rev = summer_peak[summer_peak['is_canceled'] == 1]['total_revenue'].sum()
    realized_rev = summer_peak[summer_peak['is_canceled'] == 0]['total_revenue'].sum()

    # Create comprehensive visualization
    fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(16, 12))

    # 1. Summer revenue comparison
    revenue_data = pd.DataFrame({
        'Status': ['Realized Revenue', 'Lost Revenue (Cancellations)'],
        'Amount': [realized_rev, lost_rev]
    })

    ax1 = axes[0, 0]
    bars = ax1.bar(revenue_data['Status'], revenue_data['Amount'], 
                   color=['#2ECC71', '#E74C3C'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    ax1.set_title('Summer Peak Revenue Impact (July - August)', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
    ax1.set_ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
    ax1.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.EngFormatter(unit='€'))

    # Add percentage labels
    total = realized_rev + lost_rev
    for i, (bar, pct) in enumerate(zip(bars, [realized_rev/total*100, lost_rev/total*100])):
        height = bar.get_height()
        ax1.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2., height + total*0.02,
                 f'{pct:.1f}%\n€{height:,.0f}', ha='center', va='bottom', 
                 fontweight='bold', fontsize=10)

    # 2. Monthly revenue trends
    monthly_revenue = df.groupby('arrival_date_month').agg({
        'total_revenue': 'sum',
        'is_canceled': lambda x: (x == 1).sum()
    }).reset_index()
    monthly_revenue['loss_ratio'] = monthly_revenue['is_canceled'] / (monthly_revenue['is_canceled'] + monthly_revenue['total_revenue']) * 100

    month_order = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June',
                   'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December']
    monthly_revenue['arrival_date_month'] = pd.Categorical(monthly_revenue['arrival_date_month'], categories=month_order, ordered=True)
    monthly_revenue = monthly_revenue.sort_values('arrival_date_month')

    ax2 = axes[0, 1]
    ax2_twin = ax2.twinx()
    
    line1 = ax2.plot(monthly_revenue['arrival_date_month'], monthly_revenue['total_revenue'], 
                     marker='o', color='#3498DB', linewidth=2, label='Total Revenue')
    line2 = ax2_twin.plot(monthly_revenue['arrival_date_month'], monthly_revenue['loss_ratio'], 
                          marker='s', color='#E74C3C', linewidth=2, label='Loss Ratio (%)')
    
    ax2.set_title('Monthly Revenue vs Loss Ratio', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
    ax2.set_ylabel('Total Revenue (€)', color='#3498DB')
    ax2_twin.set_ylabel('Loss Ratio (%)', color='#E74C3C')
    ax2.tick_params(axis='x', rotation=45)
    
    # Combine legends
    lines1, labels1 = ax2.get_legend_handles_labels()
    lines2, labels2 = ax2_twin.get_legend_handles_labels()
    ax2.legend(lines1 + lines2, labels1 + labels2, loc='upper left')

    # 3. Revenue by market segment
    segment_revenue = df[df['is_canceled'] == 0].groupby('market_segment')['total_revenue'].sum().sort_values(ascending=False)
    
    ax3 = axes[1, 0]
    bars = ax3.bar(segment_revenue.index, segment_revenue.values, 
                   color=plt.cm.viridis(np.linspace(0, 1, len(segment_revenue))), 
                   edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    ax3.set_title('Revenue by Market Segment (Successful Bookings)', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
    ax3.set_ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
    ax3.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.EngFormatter(unit='€'))
    ax3.tick_params(axis='x', rotation=45)
    
    # Add value labels
    for bar, val in zip(bars, segment_revenue.values):
        height = bar.get_height()
        ax3.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2., height + segment_revenue.max()*0.02,
                 f'€{val/1e6:.1f}M', ha='center', va='bottom', fontweight='bold', fontsize=9)

    # 4. Lead time vs revenue loss
    lead_time_bins = pd.cut(df['lead_time'], bins=[0, 30, 90, 180, 365, 1000],
                           labels=['Last Minute', 'Short-term', 'Medium-term', 'Long-term', 'Early Bird'])
    
    lead_revenue = df.groupby(lead_time_bins, observed=True).agg({
        'total_revenue': lambda x: x[df.loc[x.index, 'is_canceled'] == 0].sum(),
        'is_canceled': lambda x: (x == 1).sum()
    }).reset_index()
    lead_revenue['loss_ratio'] = lead_revenue['is_canceled'] / (lead_revenue['is_canceled'] + lead_revenue['total_revenue']) * 100

    ax4 = axes[1, 1]
    bars = ax4.bar(range(len(lead_revenue)), lead_revenue['loss_ratio'], 
                   color='#F39C12', edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    ax4.set_title('Revenue Loss Ratio by Lead Time Category', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
    ax4.set_ylabel('Loss Ratio (%)')
    ax4.set_xticks(range(len(lead_revenue)))
    ax4.set_xticklabels(lead_revenue['lead_time'], rotation=45)
    
    # Add value labels
    for i, (bar, val) in enumerate(zip(bars, lead_revenue['loss_ratio'])):
        height = bar.get_height()
        ax4.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2., height + 1,
                 f'{val:.1f}%', ha='center', va='bottom', fontweight='bold')

    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('advanced_revenue_analysis.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()

    # Print summary statistics
    print(f"📈 Total Potential Summer Revenue: €{realized_rev + lost_rev:,.2f}")
    print(f"💰 Actual Revenue Realized: €{realized_rev:,.2f}")
    print(f"❌ Revenue Lost: €{lost_rev:,.2f} ({(lost_rev/(realized_rev + lost_rev))*100:.2f}%)")
    
    return realized_rev, lost_rev

# Execute advanced revenue analysis
print("🔍 Analyzing detailed revenue loss patterns...")
summer_realized, summer_lost = analyze_revenue_loss(df_clean)
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 8: INTERACTIVE EXPLORATION WIDGETS
# =============================================================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("🎛️ INTERACTIVE EXPLORATION DASHBOARD")
print("="*80)
print("💡 Use the widgets below to explore different aspects of the data")
print("="*80)

# Create interactive widgets for data exploration
def interactive_hotel_analysis(hotel_type='City Hotel', metric='adr', top_n=10):
    """
    Interactive function to explore hotel data based on user selections.
    """
    # Filter data by hotel type
    hotel_data = df_clean[df_clean['hotel'] == hotel_type]
    
    print(f"\n📊 Analysis for {hotel_type}")
    print("-" * 50)
    
    if metric == 'adr':
        # Top countries by ADR
        country_adr = hotel_data.groupby('country')['adr'].mean().sort_values(ascending=False).head(top_n)
        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Average Daily Rate:")
        for country, adr in country_adr.items():
            print(f"  {country}: €{adr:.2f}")
            
        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        country_adr.plot(kind='bar', color='#3498DB', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by ADR - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Average Daily Rate (€)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()
        
    elif metric == 'revenue':
        # Top countries by total revenue
        country_revenue = hotel_data.groupby('country')['total_revenue'].sum().sort_values(ascending=False).head(top_n)
        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Total Revenue:")
        for country, revenue in country_revenue.items():
            print(f"  {country}: €{revenue:,.2f}")
            
        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        country_revenue.plot(kind='bar', color='#2ECC71', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by Revenue - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()
        
    elif metric == 'cancellation':
        # Cancellation rates by country
        country_cancel = hotel_data.groupby('country').agg({
            'is_canceled': ['sum', 'count']
        }).reset_index()
        country_cancel.columns = ['country', 'canceled', 'total']
        country_cancel['cancel_rate'] = (country_cancel['canceled'] / country_cancel['total']) * 100
        country_cancel = country_cancel[country_cancel['total'] >= 50].sort_values('cancel_rate', ascending=False).head(top_n)
        
        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Cancellation Rate (min 50 bookings):")
        for _, row in country_cancel.iterrows():
            print(f"  {row['country']}: {row['cancel_rate']:.1f}% ({row['canceled']}/{row['total']})")
            
        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        plt.bar(country_cancel['country'], country_cancel['cancel_rate'], 
                color='#E74C3C', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by Cancellation Rate - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

# Create interactive widgets
hotel_widget = widgets.Dropdown(
    options=['City Hotel', 'Resort Hotel'],
    value='City Hotel',
    description='Hotel Type:'
)

metric_widget = widgets.Dropdown(
    options=[('Average Daily Rate', 'adr'), 
             ('Total Revenue', 'revenue'), 
             ('Cancellation Rate', 'cancellation')],
    value='adr',
    description='Metric:'
)

top_n_widget = widgets.IntSlider(
    value=10,
    min=5,
    max=20,
    step=1,
    description='Top N:'
)

# Create interactive output
interact(interactive_hotel_analysis, 
         hotel_type=hotel_widget, 
         metric=metric_widget, 
         top_n=top_n_widget)
```

```python
# =============================================================================
# STEP 9: LOYALTY VS REVENUE ANALYSIS
# =============================================================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("🔄 LOYALTY vs REVENUE DEEP DIVE")
print("="*80)

# Loyalty vs Revenue Analysis
fig, axes = plt.subplots(2, 2, figsize=(16, 12))

# 1. Pricing strategy for new vs repeated guests
ax1 = axes[0, 0]
sns.barplot(data=df_clean, x='is_repeated_guest', y='adr', hue='hotel', palette='viridis', ax=ax1)
ax1.set_title('Pricing Strategy: New vs. Repeated Guests', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
ax1.set_xlabel('Is Repeated Guest (0 = No, 1 = Yes)')
ax1.set_ylabel('Average Daily Rate (ADR)')
ax1.legend(title='Hotel Type')

# 2. Lead time risk heatmap
df_clean['lead_time_bins'] = pd.cut(df_clean['lead_time'], bins=[0, 30, 90, 180, 365, 1000],
                                   labels=['Last Minute', 'Short-term', 'Medium-term', 'Long-term', 'Early Bird'])

lead_cancel_pivot = df_clean.pivot_table(index='lead_time_bins', columns='hotel', values='is_canceled', aggfunc='mean')

ax2 = axes[0, 1]
sns.heatmap(lead_cancel_pivot, annot=True, fmt=".2%", cmap='Reds', ax=ax2)
ax2.set_title('Cancellation Risk by Booking Window', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
ax2.set_xlabel('Hotel Type')
ax2.set_ylabel('Lead Time Category')

# 3. Repeated guest revenue contribution
repeat_revenue = df_clean.groupby('is_repeated_guest').agg({
    'total_revenue': 'sum',
    'adr': 'mean'
}).reset_index()
repeat_revenue['guest_type'] = repeat_revenue['is_repeated_guest'].map({0: 'New Guests', 1: 'Repeated Guests'})

ax3 = axes[1, 0]
bars = ax3.bar(repeat_revenue['guest_type'], repeat_revenue['total_revenue'], 
               color=['#3498DB', '#F39C12'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
ax3.set_title('Revenue Contribution by Guest Type', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
ax3.set_ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
ax3.yaxis.set_major_formatter(ticker.EngFormatter(unit='€'))

# Add percentage labels
total_revenue = repeat_revenue['total_revenue'].sum()
for bar, val in zip(bars, repeat_revenue['total_revenue']):
    height = bar.get_height()
    pct = (val / total_revenue) * 100
    ax3.text(bar.get_x() + bar.get_width()/2., height + total_revenue*0.02,
             f'€{val/1e6:.1f}M\n({pct:.1f}%)', ha='center', va='bottom', fontweight='bold')

# 4. Customer lifetime value proxy
# Calculate average revenue per guest by their repeat status
clv_data = df_clean.groupby(['is_repeated_guest', 'hotel']).agg({
    'total_revenue': 'mean',
    'lead_time': 'mean',
    'total_of_special_requests': 'mean'
}).reset_index()

clv_data['guest_type'] = clv_data['is_repeated_guest'].map({0: 'New', 1: 'Repeat'})
clv_data['hotel_type'] = clv_data['hotel']

ax4 = axes[1, 1]
sns.scatterplot(data=clv_data, x='total_revenue', y='lead_time', 
                hue='guest_type', size='total_of_special_requests', 
                sizes=(100, 400), ax=ax4)
ax4.set_title('Customer Value Profile (Revenue vs Lead Time)', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
ax4.set_xlabel('Average Revenue per Booking (€)')
ax4.set_ylabel('Average Lead Time (Days)')

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('loyalty_revenue_analysis.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# Print loyalty insights
repeat_stats = df_clean.groupby('is_repeated_guest').agg({
    'total_revenue': ['sum', 'mean', 'count'],
    'adr': 'mean',
    'lead_time': 'mean',
    'is_canceled': 'mean'
}).round(2)

print("\n📊 Loyalty Statistics Summary:")
print("-" * 50)
print("New Guests vs Repeated Guests:")
print(repeat_stats)
```

# =============================================================================
# STEP 8: EXPLORATORY DATA ANALYSIS (EDA)
# =============================================================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("📈 EXPLORATORY DATA ANALYSIS")
print("="*80)

# Define month_order here for consistency and self-contained execution
month_order = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June',
               'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December']

# 8.1 Cancellation Rate: New vs Repeated Guests
# Safely get cancellation rates for new and repeated guests
cancellation_by_guest_type = df_clean.groupby('is_repeated_guest')['is_canceled'].mean() * 100

new_guest_rate = cancellation_by_guest_type.get(0.0, 0.0) # Get rate for new guests (0.0), default to 0 if not found
repeated_guest_rate = cancellation_by_guest_type.get(1.0, 0.0) # Get rate for repeated guests (1.0), default to 0 if not found

print("\n1️⃣ Cancellation Rate: New vs Repeated Guests:")
print("-"*80)
print(f"✓ New Guests Cancellation Rate: {new_guest_rate:.2f}%")
print(f"✓ Repeated Guests Cancellation Rate: {repeated_guest_rate:.2f}%")

# Prepare data for plotting based on what's available
plot_labels = []
plot_rates = []
plot_colors = []

# Check if there are new guests in the dataframe
if 0.0 in df_clean['is_repeated_guest'].values:
    plot_labels.append('New Guests')
    plot_rates.append(new_guest_rate)
    plot_colors.append('#E74C3C') # Color for New Guests

# Check if there are repeated guests in the dataframe
if 1.0 in df_clean['is_repeated_guest'].values:
    plot_labels.append('Repeated Guests')
    plot_rates.append(repeated_guest_rate)
    plot_colors.append('#2ECC71') # Color for Repeated Guests

if plot_rates: # Only plot if there's data to avoid empty plot
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
    plt.bar(plot_labels, plot_rates,
            color=plot_colors, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    plt.title('Cancellation Rate: New vs Repeated Guests', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
    plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)', fontsize=12)
    plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
    for i, v in enumerate(plot_rates):
        plt.text(i, v + 1, f'{v:.2f}%', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('repeated_guests_cancellation.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
elif not df_clean['is_repeated_guest'].empty:
    # If only one type of guest (e.g., all new guests), plot that one
    if plot_labels: # Should be true if at least new guests exist
        plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
        plt.bar(plot_labels, plot_rates, color=plot_colors, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
        plt.title(f'Cancellation Rate: {plot_labels[0]}', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
        plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)', fontsize=12)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        for i, v in enumerate(plot_rates):
            plt.text(i, v + 1, f'{v:.2f}%', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.savefig(f'{plot_labels[0].lower().replace(" ", "_")}_cancellation.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
        plt.show()
else:
    print("No guest data available to plot cancellation rates for new or repeated guests.")

# 8.1 Hotel Type Distribution (Moved from previous EDA section for contextual plotting)
hotel_counts = df_clean['hotel'].value_counts()
print("\n1️⃣ Hotel Type Distribution:")
print("-"*80)
print(hotel_counts)

colors = ['#FF6B6B', '#4ECDC4']
plt.bar(hotel_counts.index, hotel_counts.values, color=colors, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
plt.title('Hotel Type Distribution', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Hotel Type', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Number of Bookings', fontsize=12)
plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(hotel_counts.values):
    plt.text(i, v + 1000, f'{v:,}', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('hotel_type_distribution.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.2 Cancellation Analysis
print("\n2️⃣ Cancellation Rate Analysis:")
print("-"*80)
cancellation_counts = df_clean['is_canceled'].value_counts()
cancellation_rate = (cancellation_counts[1] / len(df_clean) * 100)
print(f"✓ Bookings Not Canceled: {cancellation_counts[0]:,} ({100-cancellation_rate:.2f}%)")
print(f"✓ Bookings Canceled: {cancellation_counts[1]:,} ({cancellation_rate:.2f}%)")

fig, axes = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(15, 6))

# Count plot
axes[0].bar(['Not Canceled', 'Canceled'], cancellation_counts.values,
            color=['#2ECC71', '#E74C3C'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
axes[0].set_title('Cancellation Status Count', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
axes[0].set_ylabel('Number of Bookings', fontsize=11)
axes[0].grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(cancellation_counts.values):
    axes[0].text(i, v + 1000, f'{v:,}', ha='center', fontweight='bold')

# Pie chart
axes[1].pie(cancellation_counts.values, labels=['Not Canceled', 'Canceled'],
            autopct='%1.1f%%', colors=['#2ECC71', '#E74C3C'],
            startangle=90, textprops={'fontsize': 12, 'fontweight': 'bold'})
axes[1].set_title('Cancellation Rate Distribution', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')

plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('cancellation_analysis.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.3 Cancellation by Hotel Type
print("\n3️⃣ Cancellation Rate by Hotel Type:")
print("-"*80)
cancellation_by_hotel = df_clean.groupby('hotel')['is_canceled'].agg(['sum', 'count', 'mean'])
cancellation_by_hotel.columns = ['Canceled', 'Total', 'Cancellation_Rate']
cancellation_by_hotel['Cancellation_Rate'] = cancellation_by_hotel['Cancellation_Rate'] * 100
print(cancellation_by_hotel)

plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.bar(cancellation_by_hotel.index, cancellation_by_hotel['Cancellation_Rate'],
        color=['#FF6B6B', '#4ECDC4'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
plt.title('Cancellation Rate by Hotel Type', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Hotel Type', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)', fontsize=12)
plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(cancellation_by_hotel['Cancellation_Rate']):
    plt.text(i, v + 1, f'{v:.1f}%', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('cancellation_by_hotel.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.4 Monthly Booking Trends
print("\n4️⃣ Monthly Booking Trends:")
print("-"*80)
month_order = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June',
               'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December']
monthly_bookings = df_clean['arrival_date_month'].value_counts().reindex(month_order)
print(monthly_bookings)

plt.figure(figsize=(14, 6))
plt.plot(month_order, monthly_bookings.values, marker='o', linewidth=2.5,
         markersize=10, color='#3498DB', markerfacecolor='#E74C3C', markeredgewidth=2)
plt.title('Monthly Booking Trends', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Month', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Number of Bookings', fontsize=12)
plt.xticks(rotation=45, ha='right')
plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(monthly_bookings.values):
    plt.text(i, v + 300, f'{v:,}', ha='center', fontsize=9, fontweight='bold')
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('monthly_booking_trends.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.5 Average Daily Rate by Hotel Type
print("\n5️⃣ Average Daily Rate (ADR) by Hotel Type:")
print("-"*80)
adr_stats = df_clean.groupby('hotel')['adr'].describe() # Changed 'average_daily_rate' to 'adr'
print(adr_stats)

plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
df_clean.boxplot(column='adr', by='hotel', patch_artist=True, figsize=(12, 6)) # Changed 'average_daily_rate' to 'adr'
plt.title('ADR Distribution by Hotel Type', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.suptitle('')
plt.xlabel('Hotel Type', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Average Daily Rate (€)', fontsize=12)
plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('adr_by_hotel.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.6 Lead Time Distribution
print("\n6️⃣ Lead Time Distribution:")
print("-"*80)
lead_time_stats = df_clean['lead_time'].describe()
print(lead_time_stats)

plt.figure(figsize=(14, 6))
plt.hist(df_clean['lead_time'], bins=50, color='#9B59B6', edgecolor='black', alpha=0.7)
plt.title('Lead Time Distribution', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Lead Time (Days)', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Frequency', fontsize=12)
plt.axvline(df_clean['lead_time'].median(), color='red', linestyle='--',
            linewidth=2, label=f'Median: {df_clean["lead_time"].median():.0f} days')
plt.legend(fontsize=11)
plt.grid(True, alpha=0.3)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('lead_time_distribution.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 8.13 Repeated Guests Analysis
print("\n1️⃣3️⃣ Repeated Guests Analysis:")
print("-"*80)
repeated_guests = df_clean['is_repeated_guest'].value_counts()
# Ensure 1.0 is handled even if not present, default to 0 for calculation
repeated_count = repeated_guests.get(1.0, 0)
new_guest_count = repeated_guests.get(0.0, 0)

total_guests_for_rate = new_guest_count + repeated_count
repeated_rate = (repeated_count / total_guests_for_rate * 100) if total_guests_for_rate > 0 else 0.0

print(f"✓ New Guests: {new_guest_count:,} ({100-repeated_rate:.2f}%)")
print(f"✓ Repeated Guests: {repeated_count:,} ({repeated_rate:.2f}%)")

# Dynamic plot data for repeated guests analysis
plot_labels_rg = []
plot_values_rg = []
plot_colors_rg = []

if new_guest_count > 0:
    plot_labels_rg.append('New Guests')
    plot_values_rg.append(new_guest_count)
    plot_colors_rg.append('#3498DB')

if repeated_count > 0:
    plot_labels_rg.append('Repeated Guests')
    plot_values_rg.append(repeated_count)
    plot_colors_rg.append('#F39C12')

if plot_values_rg:
    plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
    plt.bar(plot_labels_rg, plot_values_rg,
            color=plot_colors_rg, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    plt.title('New vs Repeated Guests', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
    plt.ylabel('Number of Guests', fontsize=12)
    plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
    for i, v in enumerate(plot_values_rg):
        plt.text(i, v + 1000, f'{v:,}', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('repeated_guests_analysis.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
else:
    print("No guest data available for New vs Repeated Guests analysis.")

plt.title('Cancellation Rate by Deposit Type', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Deposit Type', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)', fontsize=12)
plt.xticks(rotation=15, ha='right')
plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(deposit_cancel.values):
    plt.text(i, v + 2, f'{v:.1f}%', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=10)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('deposit_type_cancellation.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 10.9 Yearly Trends

# 10.10 Country-wise Cancellation Rate

         color=plt.cm.RdYlGn_r(np.linspace(0.2, 0.8, len(country_cancel))),

# STEP 9: CORRELATION ANALYSIS
# =============================================================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("🔗 CORRELATION ANALYSIS")
print("="*80)

# Select numeric columns for correlation
numeric_cols = df_clean.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns.tolist()
correlation_matrix = df_clean[numeric_cols].corr()

# Plot correlation heatmap
plt.figure(figsize=(16, 12))
sns.heatmap(correlation_matrix, annot=True, fmt='.2f', cmap='coolwarm',
            center=0, linewidths=0.5, cbar_kws={"shrink": 0.8})
plt.title('Correlation Heatmap - All Numeric Features', fontsize=18, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('correlation_heatmap.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# Top correlations with cancellation
print("\n📊 Top Correlations with Cancellation:")
print("-"*80)
cancellation_corr = correlation_matrix['is_canceled'].sort_values(ascending=False)
print(cancellation_corr)

# Plot top correlations
top_corr = cancellation_corr[1:11]  # Exclude is_canceled itself
plt.figure(figsize=(12, 7))
colors = ['#2ECC71' if x > 0 else '#E74C3C' for x in top_corr.values]
plt.barh(top_corr.index, top_corr.values, color=colors, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
plt.title('Top 10 Features Correlated with Cancellation', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Correlation Coefficient', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Features', fontsize=12)
plt.axvline(x=0, color='black', linestyle='-', linewidth=1)
plt.grid(axis='x', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(top_corr.values):
    plt.text(v + 0.01 if v > 0 else v - 0.01, i, f'{v:.3f}',
             va='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=10)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('top_correlations_cancellation.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 10.1 Revenue Analysis by Hotel Type
print("\n1️⃣ Revenue Analysis by Hotel Type:")
print("-"*80)
revenue_by_hotel = df_clean.groupby('hotel').agg({
    'revenue': ['sum', 'mean', 'median'],
    'adr': ['mean', 'median'],
    'total_nights': 'mean'
}).round(2)
print(revenue_by_hotel)

# Revenue comparison
total_revenue = df_clean.groupby('hotel')['revenue'].sum()
plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))
plt.bar(total_revenue.index, total_revenue.values,
        color=['#FF6B6B', '#4ECDC4'], edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
plt.title('Total Revenue by Hotel Type', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
plt.xlabel('Hotel Type', fontsize=12)
plt.ylabel('Total Revenue (€)', fontsize=12)
plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
for i, v in enumerate(total_revenue.values):
    plt.text(i, v + 50000, f'€{v/1e6:.2f}M', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
plt.tight_layout()
plt.savefig('revenue_by_hotel.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
plt.show()

# 10.7 Repeated Guests vs Cancellation
print("\n7️⃣ Repeated Guests Cancellation Rate:")
print("-"*80)
repeat_cancel = df_clean.groupby('is_repeated_guest')['is_canceled'].mean() * 100

# Safely access rates to prevent KeyError if one category is missing
new_guests_cancel_rate = repeat_cancel.get(0.0, 0.0) # Default to 0.0 if not found
repeated_guests_cancel_rate = repeat_cancel.get(1.0, 0.0) # Default to 0.0 if not found

print(f"✓ New Guests Cancellation Rate: {new_guests_cancel_rate:.2f}%")
print(f"✓ Repeated Guests Cancellation Rate: {repeated_guests_cancel_rate:.2f}%")

plt.figure(figsize=(10, 6))

# Prepare data for plotting based on what's available
plot_labels_repeat = []
plot_rates_repeat = []
plot_colors_repeat = []

if 0.0 in repeat_cancel.index:
    plot_labels_repeat.append('New Guests')
    plot_rates_repeat.append(new_guests_cancel_rate)
    plot_colors_repeat.append('#E74C3C')

if 1.0 in repeat_cancel.index:
    plot_labels_repeat.append('Repeated Guests')
    plot_rates_repeat.append(repeated_guests_cancel_rate)
    plot_colors_repeat.append('#2ECC71')

if plot_rates_repeat: # Only plot if there's data to avoid empty plot
    plt.bar(plot_labels_repeat, plot_rates_repeat,
            color=plot_colors_repeat, edgecolor='black', linewidth=1.5)
    plt.title('Cancellation Rate by Guest Type', fontsize=16, fontweight='bold', pad=20)
    plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)', fontsize=12)
    plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
    for i, v in enumerate(plot_rates_repeat):
        plt.text(i, v + 1, f'{v:.2f}%', ha='center', fontweight='bold', fontsize=11)
    plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig('repeated_guests_cancellation_rate.png', dpi=300, bbox_inches='tight')
    plt.show()
else:
    print("No guest type data available to plot cancellation rates.")

# ==========================================
# STEP 4: PROFESSIONAL DATA CLEANING
# ==========================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("🧹 PROFESSIONAL DATA CLEANING (METHOD 4)")
print("="*80)

def professional_cleaning(data):
    """
    Professional data cleaning following industry best practices.

    Philosophy: CAP OUTLIERS, DON'T DELETE THEM
    - Preserves data integrity
    - Maintains statistical power
    - Uses business logic for missing values

    Returns:
        Cleaned DataFrame with new engineered features
    """
    df = data.copy()

    print("\n1️⃣ Semantic Mapping:")
    print("-" * 60)

    # Meal type mapping - make it readable
    meal_map = {
        "BB": "Bed & Breakfast",
        "FB": "Full Board",
        "HB": "Half Board",
        "SC": "Self Catering",
        "Undefined": "No Meal Plan"
    }
    df['meal_type'] = df['meal'].map(meal_map)
    print("✅ Meal types mapped to descriptive names")

    # Country mapping (top countries only, rest = 'Other')
    country_map = {
        'PRT': 'Portugal', 'GBR': 'United Kingdom', 'USA': 'United States',
        'ESP': 'Spain', 'IRL': 'Ireland', 'FRA': 'France', 'DEU': 'Germany',
        'ITA': 'Italy', 'NLD': 'Netherlands', 'BEL': 'Belgium', 'CHE': 'Switzerland',
        'AUT': 'Austria', 'POL': 'Poland', 'BRA': 'Brazil', 'RUS': 'Russia'
    }
    df['country_name'] = df['country'].map(country_map)
    df['country_name'].fillna('Other', inplace=True)

    print("\n2️⃣ Handle Missing Values (Logic-Based):")
    print("-" * 60)

    # Business logic: Missing children = 0 (no children in booking)
    df['children'] = df['children'].fillna(0)

    # Missing country = Unknown (data collection issue)
    df['country'] = df['country'].fillna('Unknown')

    # Missing agent = 0 (direct booking, no intermediary)
    df['agent'] = df['agent'].fillna(0).astype(int)

    print("✅ children → 0 (no children)")
    print("✅ country → 'Unknown'")
    print("✅ agent → 0 (direct booking)")

    print("\n3️⃣ Drop High-Cardinality / Low-Value Columns:")
    print("-" * 60)

    # Drop 'company' - 94% null, adds no value
    # Drop original 'meal' and 'country' - already mapped
    cols_to_drop = ['company', 'meal', 'country']
    df.drop(columns=cols_to_drop, inplace=True)
    print(f"✅ Dropped columns: {', '.join(cols_to_drop)}")

    print("\n4️⃣ Feature Engineering:")
    print("-" * 60)

    # Total nights stayed
    df['total_nights'] = df['stays_in_weekend_nights'] + df['stays_in_week_nights']

    # Total revenue (ADR × nights)
    df['revenue'] = df['adr'] * df['total_nights']

    # Total guests
    df['total_guests'] = df['adults'] + df['children'] + df['babies']

    # Has children flag (family booking indicator)
    df['has_children'] = ((df['children'] + df['babies']) > 0).astype(int)

    # Convert reservation status date to datetime
    df['reservation_status_date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['reservation_status_date'])

    # Lead time categories (booking window)
    df['lead_time_category'] = pd.cut(
        df['lead_time'],
        bins=[0, 30, 90, 180, 1000],
        labels=['Last Minute (0-30)', 'Short (31-90)', 'Medium (91-180)', 'Long (180+)']
    )

    # Customer segment
    df['customer_segment'] = df['has_children'].apply(
        lambda x: 'Family' if x == 1 else 'Non-Family'
    )

    print("✅ Created: total_nights, revenue, total_guests")
    print("✅ Created: has_children, customer_segment")
    print("✅ Created: lead_time_category (4 booking windows)")

    print("\n5️⃣ Remove ONLY Invalid Entries:")
    print("-" * 60)

    # Remove bookings with zero guests (data error)
    before = len(df)
    df = df[df['total_guests'] > 0]

    # Remove negative ADR (pricing error)
    df = df[df['adr'] >= 0]

    removed = before - len(df)
    retention_rate = (len(df) / before) * 100

    print(f"✅ Removed {removed:,} invalid rows (zero guests or negative ADR)")
    print(f"✅ Data retention: {retention_rate:.2f}%")

    return df

# Apply professional cleaning
df_clean = professional_cleaning(df)

print("\n" + "="*80)
print(f"📊 CLEANED DATASET: {len(df_clean):,} rows × {df_clean.shape[1]} columns")
print("="*80)

# ==========================================
# STEP 5: OUTLIER MANAGEMENT (CAPPING)
# ==========================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("📊 OUTLIER MANAGEMENT - CAPPING (NOT DELETING)")
print("="*80)

def handle_outliers_capping(data, col, percentile=0.99):
    """
    Caps outliers at specified percentile instead of removing them.

    Why capping?
    - Luxury bookings with high ADR are legitimate, not errors
    - Long lead times (advance bookings) are real business cases
    - Removing outliers loses valuable insights

    Args:
        data: DataFrame
        col: Column name to cap
        percentile: Upper percentile to cap at (default 99th)

    Returns:
        DataFrame with capped values
    """
    upper_limit = data[col].quantile(percentile)
    lower_limit = data[col].quantile(1 - percentile)

    # Count values being capped
    capped_upper = (data[col] > upper_limit).sum()
    capped_lower = (data[col] < lower_limit).sum()

    # Apply capping
    data[col] = np.where(data[col] > upper_limit, upper_limit, data[col])
    data[col] = np.where(data[col] < lower_limit, lower_limit, data[col])

    print(f"\n📌 '{col}' Outlier Capping:")
    print(f"   • Capped {capped_upper:,} high outliers (>{upper_limit:.2f})")
    print(f"   • Capped {capped_lower:,} low outliers (<{lower_limit:.2f})")
    print(f"   • New range: [{lower_limit:.2f}, {upper_limit:.2f}]")

    return data

# Cap outliers in critical columns
print("\n🎯 Capping outliers in 'adr' and 'lead_time':")
print("-" * 80)

df_clean = handle_outliers_capping(df_clean, 'adr', percentile=0.99)
df_clean = handle_outliers_capping(df_clean, 'lead_time', percentile=0.99)

# Final dataset stats
original_count = len(df)
final_count = len(df_clean)
retention = (final_count / original_count) * 100

print("\n" + "="*80)
print(f"✅ FINAL DATASET: {final_count:,} rows ({retention:.1f}% retention)")
print(f"✅ Data preservation approach: Maximum retention with quality")
print("="*80)

   • Capped 69 high outliers (>240.00)
   • Capped 0 low outliers (<0.00)
   • New range: [0.00, 240.00]

   • New range: [0.00, 322.00]

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("📊 STEP 8: CORRELATION & STATISTICAL ANALYSIS")
print("="*80)

print("""
Let's uncover relationships between variables using advanced statistical
techniques. This helps identify what truly drives cancellations and revenue.
""")

# 8.1 Correlation Matrix
print("\n1️⃣ Correlation Matrix Analysis:")
print("-"*80)

numeric_cols = df_clean.select_dtypes(include=[np.number]).columns.tolist()
correlation_matrix = df_clean[numeric_cols].corr()

# Interactive Heatmap
fig = go.Figure(data=go.Heatmap(
    z=correlation_matrix.values,
    x=correlation_matrix.columns,
    y=correlation_matrix.columns,
    colorscale='RdBu',
    zmid=0,
    text=correlation_matrix.values,
    texttemplate='%{text:.2f}',
    textfont={"size": 8},
    colorbar=dict(title='Correlation'),
    hovertemplate='%{y} vs %{x}<br>Correlation: %{z:.3f}<extra></extra>'
))

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '🔗 Correlation Matrix<br><sub>Relationships Between Numeric Variables</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 24, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    height=800,
    width=900,
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    font=dict(size=10, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.write_html('interactive_correlation_matrix.html')
fig.show()

print("✅ Saved: interactive_correlation_matrix.html")


# 8.2 Top Correlations with Cancellation
print("\n2️⃣ Features Most Correlated with Cancellation:")
print("-"*80)

cancellation_corr = correlation_matrix['is_canceled'].sort_values(ascending=False)
print(cancellation_corr)

# Top 10 correlations (excluding is_canceled itself)
top_corr = cancellation_corr[1:11]

# Interactive Bar Chart
fig = go.Figure()

colors = ['#2ECC71' if x > 0 else '#E74C3C' for x in top_corr.values]

fig.add_trace(go.Bar(
    y=top_corr.index,
    x=top_corr.values,
    orientation='h',
    text=top_corr.values,
    texttemplate='%{text:.3f}',
    textposition='outside',
    marker=dict(color=colors, line=dict(color='#2C3E50', width=1.5)),
    hovertemplate='<b>%{y}</b><br>Correlation: %{x:.3f}<extra></extra>'
))

fig.add_vline(x=0, line_dash='solid', line_color='black', line_width=2)

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '🎯 Top 10 Correlations with Cancellation<br><sub>Key Cancellation Drivers</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 24, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    xaxis_title='Correlation Coefficient',
    yaxis_title='Features',
    plot_bgcolor='#F8F9FA',
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    height=500,
    font=dict(size=12, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.write_html('interactive_cancellation_correlations.html')
fig.show()

print("✅ Saved: interactive_cancellation_correlations.html")


# 8.3 Chi-Square Test for Categorical Variables
print("\n3️⃣ Chi-Square Tests (Categorical vs Cancellation):")
print("-"*80)

categorical_features = ['hotel', 'market_segment', 'distribution_channel',
                        'customer_type', 'deposit_type', 'customer_segment']

chi_square_results = []

for feature in categorical_features:
    contingency_table = pd.crosstab(df_clean[feature], df_clean['is_canceled'])
    chi2, p_value, dof, expected = chi2_contingency(contingency_table)

    chi_square_results.append({
        'Feature': feature,
        'Chi-Square': chi2,
        'P-Value': p_value,
        'Significant': 'Yes ✓' if p_value < 0.05 else 'No ✗'
    })

    print(f"\n{feature}:")
    print(f"  Chi-Square Statistic: {chi2:.2f}")
    print(f"  P-Value: {p_value:.6f}")
    print(f"  Relationship Significant: {'Yes ✓' if p_value < 0.05 else 'No ✗'}")

chi_square_df = pd.DataFrame(chi_square_results)
print("\n📊 Summary Table:")
print(chi_square_df.to_string(index=False))

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("✅ STEP 8 COMPLETE: Statistical Analysis Finished")
print("="*80)

# 9.2 Monthly Revenue & Cancellation Trends

print("-"*80)

month_order = ['January', 'February', 'March', 'April', 'May', 'June',
               'July', 'August', 'September', 'October', 'November', 'December']

monthly_metrics = df_clean.groupby('arrival_date_month').agg({
    'revenue': 'sum',
    'is_canceled': 'mean'
}).reindex(month_order)

monthly_metrics['cancellation_rate'] = monthly_metrics['is_canceled'] * 100

print(monthly_metrics)

# Interactive Dual-Axis Chart
fig = make_subplots(specs=[[{"secondary_y": True}]])

# Revenue bars
fig.add_trace(
    go.Bar(
        x=month_order,
        y=monthly_metrics['revenue'],
        name='Revenue',
        marker=dict(color='#2ECC71', line=dict(color='#2C3E50', width=1)),
        hovertemplate='%{x}<br>Revenue: €%{y:,.0f}<extra></extra>'
    ),
    secondary_y=False
)

# Cancellation rate line
fig.add_trace(
    go.Scatter(
        x=month_order,
        y=monthly_metrics['cancellation_rate'],
        name='Cancellation Rate',
        mode='lines+markers',
        line=dict(color='#E74C3C', width=4),
        marker=dict(size=10, symbol='diamond'),
        hovertemplate='%{x}<br>Cancellation: %{y:.1f}%<extra></extra>'
    ),
    secondary_y=True
)

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '📊 Monthly Revenue vs Cancellation Rate<br><sub>Dual-Metric Trend Analysis</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 24, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    xaxis_title='Month',
    plot_bgcolor='#F8F9FA',
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    height=500,
    hovermode='x unified',
    font=dict(size=12, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Revenue (€)', secondary_y=False)
fig.update_yaxes(title_text='Cancellation Rate (%)', secondary_y=True)

fig.write_html('interactive_monthly_dual_metrics.html')
fig.show()

print("✅ Saved: interactive_monthly_dual_metrics.html")


# 9.3 Lead Time Impact Analysis
print("\n3️⃣ Lead Time Category Impact on Cancellations:")
print("-"*80)

lead_time_impact = df_clean.groupby('lead_time_category').agg({
    'is_canceled': ['mean', 'count'],
    'revenue': 'mean',
    'adr': 'mean'
}).round(2)

lead_time_impact.columns = ['Cancellation_Rate', 'Bookings', 'Avg_Revenue', 'Avg_ADR']
lead_time_impact['Cancellation_Rate'] *= 100

print(lead_time_impact)

# Interactive Multi-bar Chart
lead_categories = ['Last Minute (0-30)', 'Short (31-90)', 'Medium (91-180)', 'Long (180+)']
lead_time_impact_ordered = lead_time_impact.reindex(lead_categories)

fig = go.Figure()

fig.add_trace(go.Bar(
    x=lead_categories,
    y=lead_time_impact_ordered['Cancellation_Rate'],
    name='Cancellation Rate (%)',
    marker=dict(color=['#2ECC71', '#F39C12', '#E67E22', '#E74C3C'],
                line=dict(color='#2C3E50', width=2)),
    text=lead_time_impact_ordered['Cancellation_Rate'],
    texttemplate='%{text:.1f}%',
    textposition='outside',
    hovertemplate='<b>%{x}</b><br>Cancellation: %{y:.1f}%<extra></extra>'
))

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '⏰ Cancellation Rate by Booking Window<br><sub>Impact of Lead Time on Cancellations</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 24, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    xaxis_title='Lead Time Category',
    yaxis_title='Cancellation Rate (%)',
    plot_bgcolor='#F8F9FA',
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    height=500,
    font=dict(size=12, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.write_html('interactive_lead_time_cancellation.html')
fig.show()

print("-"*80)

deposit_analysis = df_clean.groupby('deposit_type').agg({
    'is_canceled': ['mean', 'count'],
    'revenue': 'sum',
    'adr': 'mean'

fig = go.Figure()

metrics = ['Cancellation_Rate', 'Avg_ADR']
colors_palette = ['#E74C3C', '#3498DB']

for i, metric in enumerate(metrics):
    fig.add_trace(go.Bar(
        x=deposit_analysis.index,
        y=deposit_analysis[metric],
        name=metric.replace('_', ' '),
        marker=dict(color=colors_palette[i], line=dict(color='#2C3E50', width=1.5)),
        text=deposit_analysis[metric],
        texttemplate='%{text:.1f}',
        textposition='outside'
    ))

    title={

# 9.5 Geographic Analysis - Country Cancellation Rates
print("\n5️⃣ Geographic Cancellation Analysis:")
print("-"*80)

top_10_countries = df_clean['country_name'].value_counts().head(10).index
country_analysis = df_clean[df_clean['country_name'].isin(top_10_countries)].groupby('country_name').agg({
    'is_canceled': ['mean', 'count'],
    'revenue': 'sum',
    'adr': 'mean'
}).round(2)

country_analysis.columns = ['Cancellation_Rate', 'Bookings', 'Total_Revenue', 'Avg_ADR']
country_analysis['Cancellation_Rate'] *= 100
country_analysis = country_analysis.sort_values('Cancellation_Rate', ascending=True)

print(country_analysis)

# Interactive Bubble Chart
fig = go.Figure()

fig.add_trace(go.Scatter(
    x=country_analysis['Cancellation_Rate'],
    y=country_analysis['Avg_ADR'],
    mode='markers+text',
    text=country_analysis.index,
    textposition='top center',
    marker=dict(
        size=country_analysis['Bookings'] / 100,
        color=country_analysis['Cancellation_Rate'],
        colorscale='RdYlGn_r',
        showscale=True,
        colorbar=dict(title='Cancellation %'),
        line=dict(color='#2C3E50', width=2)
    ),
    hovertemplate='<b>%{text}</b><br>' +
                  'Cancellation: %{x:.1f}%<br>' +
                  'Avg ADR: €%{y:.2f}<br>' +
                  'Bookings: %{marker.size:,}<extra></extra>'
))

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '🌍 Country Analysis: Cancellation vs ADR<br><sub>Bubble Size = Number of Bookings</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 24, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    xaxis_title='Cancellation Rate (%)',
    yaxis_title='Average Daily Rate (€)',
    plot_bgcolor='#F8F9FA',
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    height=600,
    font=dict(size=12, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.write_html('interactive_country_bubble.html')
fig.show()

print("✅ Saved: interactive_country_bubble.html")

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("✅ STEP 9 COMPLETE: Business Insights Generated")
print("="*80)

# ==========================================
# STEP 10: KEY PERFORMANCE INDICATORS (KPIs)
# ==========================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("📈 STEP 10: KEY PERFORMANCE INDICATORS DASHBOARD")
print("="*80)

# Calculate comprehensive KPIs
total_bookings = len(df_clean)
total_revenue = df_clean['revenue'].sum()
avg_adr = df_clean['adr'].mean()
cancellation_rate = (df_clean['is_canceled'].sum() / len(df_clean)) * 100
avg_lead_time = df_clean['lead_time'].mean()
avg_stay_duration = df_clean['total_nights'].mean()
repeated_guest_rate = (df_clean['is_repeated_guest'].sum() / len(df_clean)) * 100
family_booking_rate = (df_clean['has_children'].sum() / len(df_clean)) * 100

# Create KPI Dashboard
kpis = {
    'Total Bookings': f'{total_bookings:,}',
    'Total Revenue': f'€{total_revenue:,.2f}',
    'Avg Daily Rate': f'€{avg_adr:.2f}',
    'Cancellation Rate': f'{cancellation_rate:.2f}%',
    'Avg Lead Time': f'{avg_lead_time:.0f} days',
    'Avg Stay Duration': f'{avg_stay_duration:.1f} nights',
    'Repeated Guest Rate': f'{repeated_guest_rate:.2f}%',
    'Family Booking Rate': f'{family_booking_rate:.2f}%'
}

print("\n🎯 OVERALL BUSINESS METRICS")
print("="*80)
for kpi, value in kpis.items():
    print(f"  {kpi:.<30} {value:>20}")

# Interactive KPI Dashboard
fig = go.Figure()

kpi_names = list(kpis.keys())
kpi_values_numeric = [
    total_bookings, total_revenue, avg_adr, cancellation_rate,
    avg_lead_time, avg_stay_duration, repeated_guest_rate, family_booking_rate
]

fig.add_trace(go.Indicator(
    mode="number+delta",
    value=total_revenue,
    title={"text": "Total Revenue<br><span style='font-size:0.8em;color:gray'>€</span>"},
    domain={'x': [0, 0.5], 'y': [0.5, 1]},
    delta={'reference': total_revenue * 0.9}
))

fig.add_trace(go.Indicator(
    mode="gauge+number",
    value=cancellation_rate,
    title={'text': "Cancellation Rate<br><span style='font-size:0.8em;color:gray'>%</span>"},
    domain={'x': [0.5, 1], 'y': [0.5, 1]},
    gauge={
        'axis': {'range': [0, 100]},
        'bar': {'color': "#E74C3C"},
        'steps': [
            {'range': [0, 30], 'color': "#2ECC71"},
            {'range': [30, 50], 'color': "#F39C12"},
            {'range': [50, 100], 'color': "#E74C3C"}
        ],
        'threshold': {
            'line': {'color': "black", 'width': 4},
            'thickness': 0.75,
            'value': 40
        }
    }
))

fig.add_trace(go.Indicator(
    mode="number",
    value=avg_adr,
    title={"text": "Avg Daily Rate<br><span style='font-size:0.8em;color:gray'>€</span>"},
    domain={'x': [0, 0.33], 'y': [0, 0.5]}
))

fig.add_trace(go.Indicator(
    mode="number",
    value=avg_lead_time,
    title={"text": "Avg Lead Time<br><span style='font-size:0.8em;color:gray'>days</span>"},
    domain={'x': [0.33, 0.66], 'y': [0, 0.5]}
))

fig.add_trace(go.Indicator(
    mode="number",
    value=avg_stay_duration,
    title={"text": "Avg Stay<br><span style='font-size:0.8em;color:gray'>nights</span>"},
    domain={'x': [0.66, 1], 'y': [0, 0.5]}
))

fig.update_layout(
    title={
        'text': '📊 Executive KPI Dashboard<br><sub>Real-Time Business Metrics</sub>',
        'x': 0.5,
        'xanchor': 'center',
        'font': {'size': 28, 'color': '#2C3E50', 'family': 'Arial Black'}
    },
    height=700,
    paper_bgcolor='white',
    font=dict(size=14, color='#2C3E50')
)

fig.write_html('interactive_kpi_dashboard.html')
fig.show()

print("✅ Saved: interactive_kpi_dashboard.html")

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("✅ STEP 10 COMPLETE: KPI Dashboard Created")
print("="*80)

# STEP 11: ACTIONABLE RECOMMENDATIONS
# ==========================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("💡 STEP 11: DATA-DRIVEN RECOMMENDATIONS")
print("="*80)

print("""
Based on the comprehensive analysis, here are my strategic recommendations:

🎯 CANCELLATION REDUCTION STRATEGIES:

1️⃣ DEPOSIT POLICY OPTIMIZATION
   📊 Finding: Non-refundable deposits have 99% cancellation rate
   💡 Action: Implement flexible deposit tiers
      • Low-risk segments: Minimal deposit (0-20%)
      • Medium-risk: Moderate deposit (20-50%)
      • High-risk (long lead time): Higher deposits (50%+)
   📈 Expected Impact: -5-10% cancellation rate

2️⃣ LEAD TIME MANAGEMENT
   📊 Finding: Bookings 180+ days ahead have highest cancellation (46%)
   💡 Action:
      • Send reminder emails at 90, 60, 30 days before arrival
      • Offer modification flexibility for long-lead bookings
      • Implement progressive payment plans
   📈 Expected Impact: -3-7% cancellation rate

3️⃣ REPEATED GUEST LOYALTY
   📊 Finding: Repeated guests cancel 14% vs 38% for new guests
   💡 Action:
      • Launch loyalty program with benefits
      • Offer cancellation insurance for repeat customers
      • Priority room selection for returning guests
   📈 Expected Impact: +5-8% repeat customer rate

💰 REVENUE OPTIMIZATION STRATEGIES:

4️⃣ DYNAMIC PRICING
   📊 Finding: August revenue €8.4M vs January €3.2M (162% difference)
   💡 Action:
      • Increase ADR 15-20% during peak months (July-August)
      • Offer packages in low season (January-March)
      • Weekend premium pricing (+10-15%)
   📈 Expected Impact: +8-12% annual revenue

5️⃣ MARKET SEGMENT FOCUS
   📊 Finding: Online TA dominates (47% of bookings)
   💡 Action:
      • Reduce commission dependency
      • Boost direct bookings with price match + 5% discount
      • Strengthen corporate partnerships
   📈 Expected Impact: +3-5% profit margin

6️⃣ UPSELLING OPPORTUNITIES
   📊 Finding: Only 4.8% bookings have special requests
   💡 Action:
      • Proactive upsell at booking (room upgrades, spa, dining)
      • Personalized offers based on customer segment
      • Package deals (room + meal + activities)
   📈 Expected Impact: +10-15% per-booking revenue

🌍 GEOGRAPHIC EXPANSION:

7️⃣ TARGET HIGH-VALUE MARKETS
   📊 Finding: Top 3 countries = 67% of all bookings
   💡 Action:
      • Diversify marketing to Germany, France, Italy
      • Localized campaigns in underrepresented regions
      • Partner with regional travel agencies
   📈 Expected Impact: +5-10% booking diversity

🏨 OPERATIONAL EXCELLENCE:

8️⃣ FAMILY-FRIENDLY POSITIONING
   📊 Finding: Only 9.4% are family bookings
   💡 Action:
      • Develop family packages (kids stay free, activities)
      • Add family rooms and amenities
      • Seasonal promotions during school holidays
   📈 Expected Impact: +15-20% family segment growth

9️⃣ DISTRIBUTION CHANNEL OPTIMIZATION
   📊 Finding: TA/TO = 82% of bookings (high commission)
   💡 Action:
      • Invest in direct booking website optimization
      • Retargeting campaigns for abandoned bookings
      • Email marketing to past guests
   📈 Expected Impact: -5-8% distribution costs

🔟 DATA-DRIVEN OPERATIONS
   📊 Finding: 58% of cancellations happen > 30 days before arrival
   💡 Action:
      • Predictive cancellation model (ML)
      • Automatic overbooking algorithm
      • Real-time inventory optimization
   📈 Expected Impact: +3-5% occupancy rate

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

📊 PROJECTED ANNUAL IMPACT SUMMARY:

If all recommendations implemented:
   • Revenue Increase: 15-22%
   • Cancellation Reduction: 8-12 percentage points
   • Profit Margin Improvement: 5-10%
   • Customer Satisfaction: +15-25%

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
""")

print("✅ STEP 11 COMPLETE: Strategic Recommendations Delivered")

# =============================================================================
# STEP 8: INTERACTIVE EXPLORATION WIDGETS
# =============================================================================

print("\n" + "="*80)
print("🎛️ INTERACTIVE EXPLORATION DASHBOARD")
print("="*80)
print("💡 Use the widgets below to explore different aspects of the data")
print("="*80)

# Create interactive widgets for data exploration
def interactive_hotel_analysis(hotel_type='City Hotel', metric='adr', top_n=10):
    """
    Interactive function to explore hotel data based on user selections.
    """
    # Filter data by hotel type
    hotel_data = df_clean[df_clean['hotel'] == hotel_type]

    print(f"\n📊 Analysis for {hotel_type}")
    print("-" * 50)

    if metric == 'adr':
        # Top countries by ADR
        country_adr = hotel_data.groupby('Country')['average_daily_rate'].mean().sort_values(ascending=False).head(top_n)
        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Average Daily Rate:")
        for country, adr in country_adr.items():
            print(f"  {country}: €{adr:.2f}")

        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        country_adr.plot(kind='bar', color='#3498DB', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by ADR - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Average Daily Rate (€)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

    elif metric == 'revenue':
        # Top countries by total revenue
        country_revenue = hotel_data.groupby('Country')['total_revenue'].sum().sort_values(ascending=False).head(top_n)
        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Total Revenue:")
        for country, revenue in country_revenue.items():
            print(f"  {country}: €{revenue:,.2f}")

        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        country_revenue.plot(kind='bar', color='#2ECC71', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by Revenue - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Total Revenue (€)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

    elif metric == 'cancellation':
        # Cancellation rates by country
        country_cancel = hotel_data.groupby('Country').agg({
            'is_canceled': ['sum', 'count']
        }).reset_index()
        country_cancel.columns = ['country', 'canceled', 'total']
        country_cancel['cancel_rate'] = (country_cancel['canceled'] / country_cancel['total']) * 100
        country_cancel = country_cancel[country_cancel['total'] >= 50].sort_values('cancel_rate', ascending=False).head(top_n)

        print(f"Top {top_n} Countries by Cancellation Rate (min 50 bookings):")
        for _, row in country_cancel.iterrows():
            print(f"  {row['country']}: {row['cancel_rate']:.1f}% ({row['canceled']}/{row['total']})")

        # Visualization
        plt.figure(figsize=(12, 6))
        plt.bar(country_cancel['country'], country_cancel['cancel_rate'],
                color='#E74C3C', edgecolor='black')
        plt.title(f'Top {top_n} Countries by Cancellation Rate - {hotel_type}', fontsize=14, fontweight='bold')
        plt.xlabel('Country')
        plt.ylabel('Cancellation Rate (%)')
        plt.xticks(rotation=45)
        plt.grid(axis='y', alpha=0.3)
        plt.tight_layout()
        plt.show()

# Create interactive widgets
hotel_widget = widgets.Dropdown(
    options=['City Hotel', 'Resort Hotel'],
    value='City Hotel',
    description='Hotel Type:'
)

metric_widget = widgets.Dropdown(
    options=[('Average Daily Rate', 'adr'),
             ('Total Revenue', 'revenue'),
             ('Cancellation Rate', 'cancellation')],
    value='adr',
    description='Metric:'
)

top_n_widget = widgets.IntSlider(
    value=10,
    min=5,
    max=20,
    step=1,
    description='Top N:'
)

# Create interactive output
interact(interactive_hotel_analysis,
         hotel_type=hotel_widget,
         metric=metric_widget,
         top_n=top_n_widget)

# 🏨 HOTEL BOOKINGS ANALYSIS
## Professional Data Analysis & Business Intelligence Project

---

**Author:** Muhammad Danish Azeem  
**Email:** danishazeem@gmail.com  
**Links:** [LinkedIn](https://www.linkedin.com/in/muhammad-danish-094481387/) | [GitHub](https://github.com/Danish12365/PROJECTS) | [Kaggle](https://www.kaggle.com/danishazeem36512)  
**Dataset:** [Hotel Booking Demand Dataset](https://www.kaggle.com/datasets/jessemostipak/hotel-booking-demand)  
**Project Type:** Advanced Data Analysis & Business Intelligence  
**Stack:** `Python`, `Pandas`, `NumPy`, `Matplotlib`, `Seaborn`, `Plotly`, `SciPy`

---

## 📋 PROJECT OVERVIEW

### 🎯 Objective

To transform **119,390+ raw booking records** into a strategic revenue roadmap. This analysis moves beyond simple charts to employ **Robust Statistical Methods**, identifying the precise drivers of cancellations and revenue leakage.

### 💼 Business Impact

* **Revenue Optimization:** Pinpointed pricing elasticity to suggest ADR adjustments for a **20-25% potential revenue lift**.
* **Cancellation Mitigation:** Identified the "180-day Danger Zone," proposing deposit structures to reduce loss by **15-20%**.
* **Market Intelligence:** Segmented "Reliable Premium" vs. "High-Risk" cohorts for surgical marketing precision.

---

## 🗂️ ABOUT THE DATA

The dataset represents real-world booking data from two distinct hotel types: a **City Hotel** and a **Resort Hotel**.

| Feature Category | Description |
|-----------------|-------------|
| **Temporal Data** | Arrival year, month, week number, and day of stay. |
| **Booking Details** | Lead time, stays in weekend vs. week nights, and distribution channels. |
| **Guest Profile** | Adults, children, babies, country of origin, and guest type. |
| **Financials** | Average Daily Rate (ADR) and deposit types. |
| **Operations** | Special requests, parking needs, and reservation status. |

> **Data Volume:** 119,390 rows × 32 columns  
> **Temporal Range:** 2015 – 2017  
> **Key Metric (ADR):** The average revenue per paid occupied room in a given period.

---

## 📊 METHODOLOGY

This project utilizes a **Conservative Data Integrity** approach to ensure the most accurate business reflection.

### 1. High-Fidelity Data Cleaning

* **Preservation vs. Deletion:** Applied Method 4 (Capping) with 99.9% retention. In high-volume hospitality, extreme values represent distinct business cases (luxury suites, VIP guests). Dropping them would bias the revenue model.
* **Invalidation Filtering:** Only physically impossible data (e.g., 0 total guests, negative ADR) was removed.

### 2. Robust Outlier Management (Winsorization)

Instead of standard IQR removal, I employed **Winsorization** to cap extreme values (ADR and Lead Time) at the 99th percentile. This neutralizes statistical noise without sacrificing "High-Value" guest data.

### 3. Statistical Inference

Visual patterns were verified using **Chi-Square Tests** and **Correlation Analysis**. All business recommendations are backed by statistical significance at p < 0.05 confidence level.

---

## 🛠️ TECHNICAL SKILLS DEMONSTRATED

* **Advanced Wrangling:** Functional programming for cleaning pipelines and semantic mapping (Meal/Country codes).
* **Exploratory Data Analysis (EDA):** Interactive visualizations using Plotly for decision-maker engagement.
* **Inferential Statistics:** Moving beyond descriptive stats to prove causation and correlation.
* **Business Intelligence:** Translating technical coefficients into ROI-driven recommendations.

---

## 🚀 LET'S BEGIN

**"Let's begin this notebook with clarity and purpose."**

I'm embarking on a journey to understand hotel booking patterns through data. This analysis will help uncover cancellation drivers, revenue opportunities, and customer behavior insights that matter to hospitality businesses.

My approach: **Clean smartly (cap, don't delete), analyze deeply, and tell the story hidden in 119,000+ booking records.**
